The forward compilation cache root is derived from TMPDIR, so on a compute node it is node-local and does not persist between jobs

default_forward_compilation_cache_root in nova/equilibrium/solve_request.py builds $TMPDIR/nova-forward-cache/user-<uid>/host-<hostname>. Every SLURM step on this cluster reports setting TMPDIR to /tmp, because the scheduler refuses the configured /run/user path and falls back. So the cache root sits on whatever /tmp the allocated node offers. Confirm before repairing whether that path is node-local here, which is the premise this node rests on and which has not been measured: it is the ordinary arrangement and the login node carries no such directory at all, but a shared /tmp would make the whole finding moot.

If it is node-local, two consequences follow and both match what the sprint has been paying. A job persists its compile only for other jobs that land on the same node, which is why the reserved single-GPU lane sometimes appears to have a warm cache and the debug partition almost never does, since the debug node varies per allocation. And a job killed by a time limit banks nothing for its successor, so a resubmission repeats the full compile. Four allocations were lost to the one-hour cap tonight, and a fifth is at 33 minutes with 26 remaining and no output since its first line; if none of them banked compile for the next, that is the mechanism rather than bad luck.

The related failure already measured is the collision: two jobs on one node share that root and abort inside the cache write. Four gates died today with Fatal Python error: Aborted in _compile_and_write_cache, one before its first test finished. The current mitigation in every brief is a per-job TMPDIR, which removes the collision and makes the non-persistence total, so the two problems currently trade against each other and the mitigation is the wrong shape for the durable fix.

What a repair has to reconcile. The cache must be shared across jobs, isolated against concurrent writers, and keyed so that a cache built for one architecture is never read by another, which is what the host component in the path was for. A root on a shared filesystem keyed by host and architecture would persist per node across jobs, while a per-job root gives isolation and no reuse; state which is chosen and why, and measure the compile cost with and without reuse on the same program so the choice rests on a number. Note also that a shared-filesystem cache on GPFS has its own hazard, since this repo has already measured that no-clobber rename is unavailable there, so a writer strategy that assumes atomic create will not hold.

Gate: the premise confirmed or refuted with the measurement that settles it, the chosen root stated with its isolation and keying argument, and a before-and-after compile wall for one program on two separate allocations showing reuse actually occurring rather than being configured. Spec-level guided; this touches every measurement node on the plan, so land it before the next wave rather than during one.

/reckon-ship playable-forward-solve §8

Landed by pfs-compilation-cache-root (commits bb647f02 to 65b5fe0c, merged 37db9fbc). Premise confirmed by measurement: the compute node's /tmp has a filesystem id distinct from the shared home, a marker written in one allocation is invisible to the next, and every SLURM step sets TMPDIR onto it. The root moves to ~/.cache/nova/forward-compilation-cache/user-/host- on the shared GPFS home (NOVA_FORWARD_COMPILATION_CACHE_ROOT overrides the base), keyed per user and per host with JAX's runtime hash keying the architecture below; the setgid per-user data tree is avoided because its unresolvable group aborts JAX's cache eviction after the first entry. Reuse measured on two allocations on one node: 59.93 s cold compile to 8.66 s warm, 28 of 28 entries reused. Four tests green. Not measured: two concurrent allocations compiling the same program on one node, opened as its own followup. Evidence anchor the-compilation-cache-persists-across-allocations.

Establish whether the returned boundary flux and the stored surface set are the same object, because three repairs moved one and not the other

This is the successor to three refused repairs and the central question for label quality. On shot 27079 at 30 ms the stored session puts the magnetic axis at 0.30564089 Wb and its outermost surface at 0.30447092 Wb, a span of 1.17 mWb. A replay of the same frame reports the read's returned boundary flux bit-identical to the wall anchor at 0.25656927 Wb on all 154 limited frames measured, and identical to the X-point on none. Those two figures are 48 mWb apart. Either the stored surface set is not placed at the returned boundary flux, or the two measurements are not of the same frame, and nobody has checked which.

It matters more than any repair proposed so far. The collapsed extent everyone has measured all evening, the four per cent enclosed area and the 142.91 mm standoff, lives on the stored surface set, which is also what the consumer renders as its conditioning raster. Every repair attempted has been aimed at the anchor selection, which governs the returned flux. If those are different objects then three green gates moving nothing is explained by one fact, and the repair target has been wrong from the start.

The measurement is one frame in one process, and it must print rather than infer: the axis flux, the boundary flux the read returns, the flux of the outermost member of the surface set the writer persists, and the normalised-flux value that surface carries. Then state plainly whether the surface set is placed at the returned boundary flux, and if not, name where its levels come from. Print the revision measured at, because a replay at a worker revision and a session written at another are two different runs and that distinction has already misled this plan twice.

Do not propose or implement a repair in this node. Answered, and the answer names a seam rather than a confusion. /reckon-ship playable-forward-solve §8

Route the writer's boundary through the read that carries the shadow, and measure the area before believing it

This is the aimed successor to three refused repairs, and it is the first one with a named seam rather than an inferred mechanism. Measured in one process on shot 27079 row 15: the public topology read returns a boundary flux 1.17 mWb from the axis, the private _read_active_partition returns one 49.07 mWb from the axis, and the writer's persisted surface set is bit-identical to the public value. The masking and class-default work altered only the private value. So the shadow machinery has been improving a boundary nobody stores.

The change is a wiring question and it must be argued rather than assumed. Establish first why two reads exist and what each is for, because a private intermediate that disagrees with the public receipt by a factor of forty-two is either a deliberate separation with a reason or an accident, and the repair differs completely between those. If it is deliberate, the fix is to make the public receipt consume the gated result; if it is accidental, the two reads should converge and one should go.

Then measure the area before believing anything. The acceptance is unchanged from the three refused attempts and is deliberately the same measure so the attempts are comparable: the first three drawn frames of 27079 moving off enclosed-area ratios 0.0418457614, 0.3353593144 and 0.5710731398 against the reconstruction, on a relabel into a fresh root, with the frame count printed beside them. A green suite is not evidence here; two of the three refused repairs passed every gate. Also required unchanged: the twelve positive-current bank rows and the Solovev certificate bit-identical, and a pinned diverted read untouched.

Three cautions from the attempts already made. Do not exclude the centre column or any structure by name; the selected node being on the lower centre column at R 0.19524 is a description of the outcome, not a licence to special-case it. Do not set a tolerance from the values currently observed. And carry the corollary measurement: if the public boundary moves, the current-centroid major radius sitting inboard of EFIT on every slice measured, and the monotonic drift of the diverted control, both become candidates for the same cause and should be re-read rather than left as separate items.

Take a fresh base at dispatch rather than merging main into a worktree, since two nodes were made unpromotable that way today. Spec-level guided; delicate topology code with corpus-wide reach, so the codex lane as the lead directed for topology work.

/reckon-ship playable-forward-solve §8

Attribute the diverted span ratio's dependence on the solenoid current

The drift diagnosis names the solenoid current as the sole per-slice quantity that beats time in explaining the diverted span ratio against EFIT (R2 0.497 against time's 0.351, correlation -0.83 over 43,011 finite rows). Establish how the solenoid's flux enters the diverted boundary span, testing the three named candidates rather than rediscovering them: the direct external-field contribution of the solenoid at the X-point and along the boundary (project the solenoid's flux image onto the span change per ampere and compare with the fitted slope); the saddle position relative to the grid's refined region as the solenoid ramps (does the X-point walk across a resolution boundary in the census rows); and the profile interpolation of p_prime and ff_prime from the reference psi_norm grid onto nova's as the profile shape evolves with the ramp. Report per candidate the fraction of the fitted slope it accounts for on the census rows, and name the carrier or state plainly that none of the three carries it. Diagnosis only; no repair; the census rows and per-slice quantities already exist under docs/figures and in the sessions, so no card is needed except for the flux-image projection, which is one CPU solve of the vacuum response.
/reckon-ship playable-forward-solve §8

Test whether the limited-boundary collapse on early frames is the free solve's vertical placement, by re-solving rows 15 to 17 of 27079 with the centroid row at EFIT's height

Four repairs of the limited anchor are now measured and refused, and the last one settles where the defect is not: ranking the wall anchor only among contained, unshadowed candidates still selects an upper-wall node (10, then 18) and moves the area ratio away from one, while the up-down mirror node 25, whose contour encloses 0.72 and 0.99 of the reconstruction on rows 16 and 17, is never the flux extremum. The anchor rule takes the first wall contact as the flux surfaces expand from the axis, which is right by definition, so an upper-wall contact on a frame whose plasma should fill the lower vessel says the flux map itself sits high. Test that directly and cheaply: re-solve rows 15, 16 and 17 (and 11 to 14 as unconverged controls) of 27079 with the vertical current-centroid row imposed at efm current_centrd_z, the conditioning the labeller already carries as its branch guard, and read from each conditioned equilibrium the solve's axis R and Z beside EFIT's rmaxis and zmaxis, the wall node of first contact with its flux distance from the axis, and the enclosed-area ratio against the reconstruction, beside the same three quantities from the free solve. If the conditioned equilibrium contacts the lower wall near node 25 at a ratio near one, the collapse is the free solve's vertical placement and the existing conditioning is the remedy for label quality on early limited frames, to be recorded on the labeller decision; if it still contacts the upper wall, the placement hypothesis is refuted and the atlas panels decide the next question. Three to seven solves on one card or a debug partition; no repair; no structure excluded by name.
/reckon-ship playable-forward-solve §8

Measure two concurrent allocations compiling the same program against the shared cache root before briefs drop the per-job TMPDIR

The shared cache root asserts that allocations on the same node are serialised by JAX's own cache entry lock, so two jobs compiling the same program at once neither abort nor corrupt an entry. That claim was not measured; the reuse receipt ran its two allocations one after the other. The earlier aborts inside the cache write (four gates dying with Fatal Python error in _compile_and_write_cache in one day) were attributed to concurrent writers on a shared root, and the per-job TMPDIR every brief carries exists to prevent exactly that. Measure it: submit two debug-partition jobs on one node at the same time, each cold-compiling the same forward program against the new root with the cache entries cleared first, capture both logs, and state whether both complete, whether either aborts, how many entries the root holds afterwards against the single-allocation count of 28, and whether the second job's compile wall shows reuse or a full recompile. If both complete cleanly the per-job TMPDIR instruction in the briefs is retired for the cache's sake; if either aborts, the root needs a per-job entry-lock strategy before the corpus relabel runs several cards on one node. Spec exact; test role; a debug partition with two simultaneous four-core jobs.
/reckon-ship playable-forward-solve §8

Measured by pfs-cache-concurrent-writers: two debug jobs pinned to one idle node cold-compiled the same forward program simultaneously against the shared root with the host subtree cleared first; both completed, neither aborted inside the cache write, and the root held 28 entries afterwards, identical to the single-allocation count. The per-job TMPDIR instruction is retired for the cache's sake and the shared root needs no entry-lock strategy for same-node writers. Where the numbers stand and how the constrained reduced route is posed. Measured on the reduced-state plain-Newton route (millisecond-converged-solve, anchor millisecond-step): accepting Newton step 2.15 ms median on 22086/43, warm trip 46 to 58 ms with 95 percent of the trip being one topology read, a converged solve in three trips. A keyframe re-solved from the previous equilibrium in one to three trips therefore sits at 50 to 150 ms before any further trip-boundary work, so the order-100 ms budget is reachable on this route and the constraint rows are what remains. The constrained reduced route appends the compensating unknowns to the amplitude vector and uses the augmented residual of assemble_augmented_system: the augmented state is [u, c] with u the plasma-cell amplitudes the frozen partition defines and c the normalised compensating unknowns, one per registered row; the reconstruction is external + sum_k flux_delta_k(c_k) + P scatter(u) behind the frozen shadow; the residual is [u - gather(moments(reconstruct)), g_k(reconstruct, c)] with each row scaled as the Newton-Krylov augmentation scales it; the dense Jacobian is one jacfwd over the augmented state per trip; the merit adds the scaled constraint residuals to the production flux merit so a step is accepted on the augmented system. Rows enter through the ConstraintFunctional protocol, so the current-centroid row on main and the bounding-box rows in flight are the same kind of input. The warm-start contract: a moved target re-solved from the previous [u, c] and shadow converges in fewer trips than the same target solved from the cold seed, and the unmoved target re-solves in one trip with zero accepted steps.

The height pin is a scoped exception with a removal trigger, on both sides. The imas-ambix lead authorised it on 2026-09-05 for the demo only; it is removed when a Thomson forward model can supply the plasma height instead of the reconstruction, and that sentence is to be written where the row is applied in the labeller, not only here, so a reader touching the pin can tell whether the condition is met. Two records travel with every dataset this section produces: the per-slice conditioning flag and target source, which is both the firewall's enforcement across the seam (neither imas-ambix guard sees a number handed over as data) and the removal mechanism (which labels carry the pin is a flag read, not a corpus audit); and the caveat that any held-out result scored by the EFIT referee on a corpus built with these labels carries a one-scalar-per-slice leak on the axis position, the interior quantity whose skill claims have been the weakest and most contested, so a demo figure quoted as skill would be flattered on exactly that number. Both facts sit beside the numbers in every receipt this section writes.

The topology read now accepts a per-wall-node private mask and applies it to the wall flux before the extremum is selected, with the previous iterate's wall shadow supplied as that mask, so the shadow finally gates the anchor it was built to constrain instead of being computed downstream of it. Masking applies only to a pinned limited read: the emergent path keeps its own saddle-height rule and a pinned diverted read is untouched by construction rather than by test. Masked nodes take a finite losing score rather than an infinity so the fixed-shape quadratic interpolation the wall reader depends on still holds; the first focused attempt passed 2 of 3 and exposed a mask-induced quadratic overshoot, and a bounded eligible-flux repair produced the green rerun, which is the subtlety this change existed to get right. Cold start is decided explicitly and documented: the first iterate keeps the unmasked historical read rather than deriving a mask circularly from the boundary it is choosing, so the repair does not address the cold-start collapse at a 0.066 median and is not claimed to. seven shots relabelled at the wall-anchor repair revision into a new root; written and converged counts match the banked sessions exactly on all seven

The relabel is done and the acceptance measure now has data to run against. Seven shots written at revision 728266be into /work/projects/imas_gpu/sophelio/labeller_sessions/boundary-repair-validation-20260907T1124Z, one H200 job, exit 0 in 8m56s, with the guard-failure conditioning on so guard-failing frames are present rather than filtered. The repair is verified present in that tree by content and not merely reachable as a commit: the private-wall mask parameter appears nine times in the topology read and the previous-shadow accessor four times in the forward operator at that exact revision.

Written and converged counts match the banked sessions exactly on all seven, 114/102, 70/46, 69/44, 67/39, 61/40, 73/45 and 57/46. That is a count-level identity and nothing more. It says the repair changed no frame's admission, which is what a boundary-selection change should do, and says nothing about the values, which is the measure that is still outstanding.

One caveat that belongs on the record because it is easy to state too strongly. The banked root was written into by a different live producer during this job's wall-clock interval: shots 15971, 16037, 16040 and 17139 gained triplets between 13:04 and 13:11. The relabel job named only the new root and created only its own twenty-one files, so its isolation holds; whole-root immutability of the banked corpus over the interval does not, and a promotion asserting it would be inaccurate. None of the four is among the seven, so the paired comparison is unaffected, but a fresh whole-corpus census would now read four shots the banked one did not.

The wall-anchor repair did not reach the frames it was written for, and the plan's earlier record of it overstates what landed. The relabelled corpus is bit-identical to the banked one on the limited class over 363 paired frames, while the diverted control moved by 1.18e-13. The solve re-ran; the mask did not fire. The repair's landing record stands as an accurate description of the code that was written, the gates it passed and the cold-start decision it documented, and it should now be read alongside the measurement rather than as a statement that the defect is repaired. It is not. The limited-boundary corruption is live in the corpus and remains the highest-value item on this plan.

Worth naming precisely why the gates did not catch it. The focused three and the referee twenty-one were unit tests on manufactured maps, where the mask does fire because the fixture supplies the qualified saddles the predicate needs. Nothing in that set exercised a real limited frame, which is the population the change existed to repair. This is the third instance today of a null reading like a pass, and the sharpest form of the rule the sprint has been converging on: an instrument must be shown to read a nonzero difference when one exists, and a gate whose fixtures guarantee the mechanism fires cannot report that the mechanism never fires in production.

One correction from the consumer, offered against its own earlier claim and worth keeping. It had said the cold frames the repair deliberately excludes are the ones its short-horizon case depends on most. On the arithmetic of seven cold frames across seven shots, one per shot, and a renderer that starts at slice five seeding from the four camera frames before it, the residual is roughly one conditioning slice in forty-six and sits adjacent to the seed window rather than inside it. The cold exception is still a real remainder; it is not the acute one.

Two measurements land together and they change the ordering: the cold frame is not a separable remainder, and the wall polygon every absolute distance rests on is now validated.

Cold first. I recorded twenty minutes ago, from the consumer's own correction, that the cold exception sits beside the seed window rather than in it and is therefore not the acute case. That is wrong and the media session has the measurement. On all seven relabelled shots, drawn frames only: the first frame drawn is cold on seven of seven, limited on seven of seven, and is the single weakest frame in its own sequence by axis-to-boundary flux span, rank one of between 33 and 81 every time. It carries 0.75 per cent of its shot median span on 27079 and 12.7 to 14.9 per cent on the six carriers. The cold flag also recurs mid-sequence, at ranks 27, 43, 50, 53, 66 and 71 on 27079, so skipping a lead frame fixes nothing.

The mechanism is definitional rather than incidental, which is why it is uniform. A cold frame is one whose stored predecessor failed the guard, and a figure or a sequence admits only guarded frames, so wherever a run opens with guard failures the first admitted frame is cold by construction. All seven do. The renderer starting at slice five is the same fact stated the other way round: slices one to four failed the guard, which is exactly what makes slice five cold. Every animation published from this corpus opens on its most collapsed frame.

The arithmetic that produced my wrong correction is worth keeping as a lesson. The one-in-forty-six figure came from the census cell counting frames that are cold AND limited AND admitted, seven across seven shots, while the drawn sequences contain twenty cold frames. A rate computed from an intersection understated the condition threefold, and the position was the load-bearing part rather than the rate. So cold is not separable from the limited defect on this evidence: the same frame is both, on every shot, and it is the frame everything downstream starts from.

Second, the reference. The consumer validated its 36-vertex wall constant against the machine description for 22086's own wall branch, compared in both directions with the machine side densely resampled at 4096 points: 37 stored nodes with a repeated endpoint, 36 effective, and a signed mismatch peaking at 0.0044 mm, worst at R 0.19524, Z -1.0835. Four microns. So the limiter contour is the machine wall, and every absolute magnitude that was provisional on it is restored: the 142.91 mm median and 391.64 mm maximum limited-boundary standoffs on 27079, the consumer's 46.3 mm median and 170 mm maximum boundary distances, the second-X-point excursions at 30.15 and 175.38 mm, and the physics half of the containment claim rather than only its behaviour.

What that validation cannot answer, and the consumer refused to answer rather than guessing, is how many limiter units MAST's raw wall IDS carries. Its path reads a level-2 store where the description was already selected upstream, and the counts that store records, one description and 37 limiter units, describe the flattening rather than the source: the 37 equals the vertex count and comes from a geometry channel wearing a units label. A reader downstream of a positional selection cannot report on that selection. The audit node reading the raw IDS owns that question and nothing should state an answer until it does.

Independent corroboration on the mask mechanism, from two readings that could not have produced each other. Reading the predicate in wall_height_shadow_mask: the exclusion band forms from qualified saddles, admitting a primary and at most one opposite-side saddle, and where neither side is valid the function returns the previous shadow unchanged. A limited frame is by definition one with no qualified saddle binding the boundary, and a cold start supplies no previous shadow by design, so on that reading the shadow starts empty and stays empty for a limited run.

That predicts firing on diverted frames, which have qualified saddles, and never on limited ones. It is exactly the asymmetry the paired measurement found from the other side: diverted warm moving 1.18e-13 while limited returns 0.000e+00. Source reading and output reading are independent and agree on a mechanism neither establishes alone. It also disposes of the upper-roof hypothesis rather than confirming it: if the predicate never reaches classification, whether the node at R 0.565, Z 1.728 counts as private was the wrong question. The census node settles it, and the deciding field is the masked-node count being zero.

The two corpus roots do not carry the same boundary quantity, and nobody planned that. Read through one tool on the steering group of both files: in the banked root lcfs_r and lcfs_z have a zero-length vertex dimension with n_boundary_coords zero on every frame, so no boundary polyline is stored at all; in the relabelled root they are populated at 65 vertices. The media reader carries a documented fallback for the empty case and takes the outermost nested flux surface instead, so every figure drawn from the banked root and every figure drawn from the relabelled root are drawing different quantities. They agree to 1e-12 where both exist, which is why nothing caught it.

This retires the earlier note that the relabelled boundary was the banked polyline plus a closing vertex. That was right about the geometry and wrong about the mechanism: it is not one variable gaining a point, it is a different variable being written at all. The consumer's independent count of 64 raw finite vertices in both roots cannot have come from lcfs_r, which is length zero on the banked side; it is almost certainly counting flux_surface_r, shape (11, 64, 114) and identical in both files. An available quantity standing in for the one asked about, agreeing perfectly and answering nothing, which is the same substitution shape as a reader downstream of the selection it was asked to report on.

The physics half is unmoved and now for a sharper reason. flux_surface_r, flux_surface_z and flux_surface_psi are bit-identical between the two roots to the last digit. So the relabel changed what the writer persists and nothing about what the solver computed, which is a third independent line on the null beside the paired bit-identity and the consumer's limiter-distance reproduction, and it predicted the census count before that count existed.

Two consequences to carry. Any comparison anyone runs across the two roots sees a boundary provenance change that is not a physics change, and the receipts already distinguish them because they report the boundary source from n_boundary_coords rather than from a constant. And the writer began persisting a boundary somewhere between the two revisions without that being part of any node's declared scope, which is worth locating: if it was intended as repair work then the repair reached the persistence layer and stopped short of the selection, which localises where to look.

the private-wall mask excludes zero wall nodes on all 154 limited frames across seven shots and never masks the winning anchor, so the predicate never fires on the class it was written for

The boundary-provenance divergence is confirmed by direct read rather than by either session's derived measure, and it holds on every shot checked. From the steering group of each file, one tool and one call: the banked root carries lcfs_r with a zero-length vertex dimension and a per-frame boundary count of zero on 27079, 21978 and 22086 alike, and the relabelled root carries 65 vertices on all three. So the banked corpus stores no boundary polyline at all, and no comparison of stored boundary lengths between the roots can return a matched pair.

A peer's contrary reading is recorded here because the way it failed is the reusable part. It reported both roots storing 64, from arrays of shape (frames, 64, 2), and the shape is the tell: flux_surface_r in those same files is (11, 64, frames), eleven nested surfaces of 64 theta points, and it is bit-identical between the roots. The read named the right group and the right files and took a neighbouring array. That is the third instance today of an available quantity standing in for the one asked about, after a limiter unit count read from a store downstream of the selection it was meant to report on, and a vertex count that normalised away the difference in question by counting only finite entries. In each case the number was correct, internally consistent and about something else, and in each case the discriminating information sat in the shape or the provenance before the value was believed.

What survives from that read is worth keeping and is not small: the flux surfaces agree between the roots to 7.34e-14 metres in raw stored coordinates. That is a third independent line on the null, in the written data rather than through anyone's derived measure, and it says the solve re-ran and produced round-off differences while the topology selection did not move.

The stored polyline is the outermost nested surface with its loop closed, measured rather than deduced, and that softens a claim of mine above. On 27079 in the relabelled root: lcfs_r is (65, 114) and flux_surface_r is (11, 64, 114); the first 64 stored vertices match nested surface index 10, the outermost, with a maximum absolute difference of exactly 0.0 in both coordinates, and the 65th vertex equals the first exactly. So the writer began emitting a closed copy of a curve the reader was already producing.

What that corrects. I recorded that figures drawn from the two roots draw different quantities. That is true of the variable and false of the geometry: the fallback surface and the stored polyline are the same curve, so no figure published today was drawing a different shape and nothing visual was wrong. The peer session reached this by deduction from two numbers we already held, reasoning that a 64-point surface and a 65-point polyline can only agree on their overlap if one is the closed form of the other, and the direct read confirms it.

What it does not change. The reader's prefer-if-present branch is still live and still unspecified, and the node opened for it stands on its own terms. The hazard was never that the two curves differ today; it is that a branch keyed on emptiness switches source per file and per frame, so nothing prevents the two from diverging tomorrow while the reader continues to report a boundary either way. A branch that currently selects between two identical things is the hardest kind to notice and the easiest to leave in.

It also strengthens the null rather than complicating it. The paired geometric comparison matched exactly on the overlap, so the repair changed no geometry, and it did so despite the comparison not being of like with like, which is a stronger result than the one originally claimed.

The batched acceptance produced real per-slice numbers for the first time, and the comparison it makes is invalid because the reference arm carries a defect this plan already documented. The engine's branch-shape fault is genuinely gone: both arms ran to completion on 48 slices in one job, with the sequential reference at 2014 s and the batched route at 215 s on three cards. No throughput figure is quoted from that pair and none should be until the arms are comparing the same work, since the earlier retraction on this node came from exactly that mistake.

The reference arm resolves its solve with an explicit class and then reads the achieved centroid without one. At benchmarks/batched_labeller_receipt.py the solve is called with requested_class=requested and the very next call, current_moment_observation with a support and a target current, passes no class at all and forwards None. That is the unguarded-sibling defect measured earlier tonight against 119 aborted corpus rows, reproduced inside the instrument that is supposed to be the trusted side of this comparison. It threw on 7 of 48 slices, six with the no-qualified-axis refusal at the guard stage and one with a concretisation error, and those slices are counted as unconverged in the reference.

So the arithmetic that failed the gate is measuring the reference. The sequential arm reports 39 converged of 48 against the batched arm's 42, and the eight failed checks are led by converged and guard fraction parity. A reference that refuses seven slices in a read the batched path does not perform cannot establish parity for anything, and the direction of the disagreement is the one that defect predicts. What the run does establish is that the engine executes, conditions, and produces finite per-slice centroid errors, most of them at the 1e-9 level with a handful in the centimetres.

Two smaller facts from the same receipt. The compilation cache directory is /tmp/job-1264383/..., confirming the per-job root now in every brief and therefore confirming that this job banked nothing for its successor. And one slice raised a concretisation error where a traced value met a float conversion, which is a separate defect from the class omission and should not be folded into it.

both labeller writers now pass the solved topology class into their achieved-centroid reads, closing the admission asymmetry that aborted 119 corpus rows

The class-default repair is the second intervention on this defect to land green and change nothing, and the worker caught it inside its own node. Its gates all passed: seven of seven focused, twenty-four of twenty-four on the wall exclusion suite, twenty-one of twenty-one on the referee including the twelve positive-current bank rows and the Solovev certificate, with a pinned diverted read unchanged. It then measured the acceptance on the frames the repair targets and reported that the boundary did not move. Enclosed-area ratios came back 0.0418457480, 0.3353593144 and 0.5710731412 against a baseline of 0.0418457614, 0.3353593144 and 0.5710731398. The middle value is identical and the others differ in the eighth digit, which is re-run round-off rather than a different selection.

The process difference from this morning is the thing to keep. The previous attempt passed a focused gate and a referee and was recorded as a repair, and it took a seven-shot relabel and a paired comparison hours later to establish it was bit-identical. This one reached the same outcome and reported it as a blocker before anyone else read it. A gate is not a measurement, and the acceptance measure belonged inside the node.

The premise nobody has tested is now the live question. Every intervention on this defect has assumed the collapsed limited boundary is set by the wall anchor: the shadow ordering repair, the masking of the wall flux, and now the class default that gates the anchor from the first iterate. Three changes to that path, three no-ops, and a census showing the mask excludes zero nodes on 154 limited frames. So establish on one collapsed frame which code path actually produces the boundary value that comes out, printing the selected anchor node with its position and flux beside the boundary flux the read returns and whether those are the same quantity. If the boundary on these frames comes from a saddle, a clip, a fallback or a prior iterate, then every repair so far has been aimed at code the frame never executes, and one fact explains three null results.

One reading to check rather than adopt, recorded because it inverts the current direction. On the worst frame the axis-to-boundary span is 1.17 mWb against a 396 mWb shot median, so the boundary flux is nearly the axis flux. If the wall extremum is genuinely near the axis flux early in a shot, with the solenoid strongest and the plasma weakest, then the anchor selection is not making an error at all and the small closed region is the correct answer to the question asked. In that case the defect would sit in pinning the class to limited for a plasma not yet in contact with anything, which is the opposite of the current direction, and the boundary-provenance measurement distinguishes the two without anyone having to argue it.

A coordinator instruction of mine made a completed node unpromotable, and the fix is a dispatch argument rather than a waiver. When the app-demo node reported its first blocker I told it to merge current main into its worktree, so that the second carrier would be measured against the sign-aware axis read and the wall-anchor change rather than against a base cut before them. That was right for the measurement and wrong for the record. The promotion boundary check reads the diff from the run's base to its head, and that span now crosses a merge of main, so it reports every path the rest of the sprint touched tonight as outside this run's declared scope: sixty of them, including three plans, the ledger, the media figures, and the writers' scripts.

The node itself is clean and this was verified per commit rather than assumed. All five of its own commits touch exactly two paths, benchmarks/playable_keyframe_receipt.py and docs/figures/playable-forward-solve/keyframes/, both declared. Everything else entered through merge commit 2fbc6089, whose second parent is the main tip of the time.

The refusal was left standing rather than cleared. --accept-path exists for a deliberate companion path, and naming sixty paths of peer work with it would write a ledger row asserting that this run changed the media session's animations and three plan documents. That record is the instrument which has repeatedly caught overstatement tonight, and a false attribution in it is worse than a missing row. The boundary waiver does not apply either, since it is scoped to an uncommitted edit in a dispatch-visible tree. So the run's result is recorded here in full, its commits are merged and pushed, and its pointer is left as a deliberate remainder rather than promoted or discarded.

The rule that follows, for every future node in this position: when a blocked run needs a newer base, redispatch or resume it at that base rather than instructing it to merge main into its worktree. A worker's worktree diff is the promotion record, so pulling main into it destroys the record's precision even though it improves the measurement. The two goals are not in tension if the base is chosen at dispatch.

The mechanism is measured and it is not what any of the three repairs assumed. Two of my own readings are wrong and the class-default direction is aimed the wrong way for these frames. One H200 job, exit zero in 616 s, over 154 limited frames of the seven relabelled shots.

The boundary does come from the wall anchor, so that premise survives. On all 154 frames the returned boundary flux is bit-identical to the wall-anchor flux, and on zero of them is it bit-identical to the X-point flux. So the provenance question is closed and every repair has been aimed at the right code.

What is wrong is my source reading that a limited frame has no qualified saddle. The predicate consumed four qualified saddles on 26 frames, five on 77, six on 49 and seven on two. It classifies, and its branches fire: on the lower side 78 frames took the qualified height band, 73 took the connectivity private fallback and three retained the previous shadow, with 70, 81 and three on the upper side. So the earlier census reading zero masked nodes was a property of the unrepaired wiring, not of the predicate, and the class default did make the mask fire.

The mask now fires hard and the boundary still does not move, because the winning node survives it. On the worst frame, shot 27079 row 15 at 30 ms, the production gate masked 33 of 37 wall nodes and still selected node 18, at R 0.19524, Z -1.0835 — the lower centre column. Its flux, 0.2565692689948401 Wb, becomes the boundary flux exactly. So this is the third outcome the diagnostic was set up to distinguish: nonzero masking with an unchanged boundary, meaning the masked nodes are not the winning node.

And the centre column is back, having been proposed and refuted twice tonight. It was refuted as a hypothesis about the mechanism and it is correct as a description of the selected node. Those are different claims and the earlier refutations were of the first.

The decisive number is the comparison the frame never makes. That frame has an X-point at 0.22084343293954597 Wb, and its read diverted flag is false. The X-point flux sits far further from the axis than the wall contact does, so an X-point boundary would enclose a sensible plasma while the centre-column contact encloses four per cent of the reconstruction area. The frame is pinned limited, so it takes the wall and never considers the null. That is the inverted reading recorded an hour ago as a thing to check rather than adopt, and it is now measured: the fault is the class, and defaulting a cold iterate to limited entrenches it rather than repairing it. The lead's directive allowed for transition into diverted where the branch permits; the measurement says the transition is not happening on the frames that need it.

Retracting the comment above: the class conclusion does not follow from the arithmetic, and the two sets of flux numbers are not describing the same quantity. Read from the stored session for shot 27079 at 30 ms: the magnetic axis sits at 0.30564089 Wb and the outermost stored surface at 0.30447092 Wb, so the boundary is 1.17 mWb from the axis, matching the collapsed span recorded all evening. The worker's measured wall anchor is 0.25656927 Wb and its X-point 0.22084343 Wb, which are 49 and 85 mWb from that axis.

So the argument I made is wrong on its own terms. Under the ordinary rule the boundary is whichever candidate lies closest to the axis in flux, and on these numbers the wall contact beats the X-point by 36 mWb. Taking the wall would be correct rather than a fault, and there is no case here for the frame having been wrongly pinned limited. That reading was recorded an hour earlier as a thing to check rather than adopt, and when the measurement arrived I adopted it without doing the subtraction.

The deeper problem is that neither measured candidate is where the stored boundary sits. The worker reports the read's returned boundary flux bit-identical to the wall anchor on all 154 frames and identical to the X-point on none, which is internally consistent for its own replay. The session stores a surface set whose outermost member is 48 mWb away from that returned value. Two quantities under one name, agreeing in the narrative and not in the arithmetic, which is the fourth instance of that shape today and the first with me as the instrument.

What is established: the collapsed extent is real and lives on the stored surface set, whose outermost member spans R 0.587 to 0.726 about an axis at R 0.658; the mask fires hard once a cold iterate defaults to limited, masking 33 of 37 nodes; the predicate does classify, consuming four to seven qualified saddles per frame; and every returned boundary flux tracks the wall anchor rather than the null. What is not established is which code path places the stored surface set 1.17 mWb from the axis, and no repair should be dispatched until it is.

The next measurement is one frame in one run, printing the read's returned boundary flux beside the stored outermost surface flux and the axis flux, and stating whether the surface set is placed at the returned boundary flux at all. If it is not, then every measurement of this defect through the stored surfaces and every measurement through the read have been describing different objects, and that alone would explain three repairs that moved the returned flux and left the stored extent untouched.

seed policy and residual tolerance are measured null; the 41.3 mm axis shift is carried by the case-state physics anchor, the frozen operator deriving p-prime and FF-prime from a calibration row rather than the slices own; grid extent unmeasured

The axis attribution lands with two measured nulls, a named mover, and a shared prior that all three sessions held and none of them tested. Five of six declared arms completed; the sixth timed out in a dense mesh profile build and is named outstanding rather than quietly dropped.

Seed policy is null. An explicit case-state seed resolves the axis at R 0.86478, Z -0.01485, identical to the labeller continuation to the seventh decimal, with a spread of 9 nm in radius and 75 nm in height across three arms. Route with residual tolerance is null. Tightening the finish from 1e-8 to 1e-12, which moved the final residual from 5.2e-9 to 4.5e-13, leaves the axis identical.

The mover is the case-state physics anchor. The 41.3 mm shift in major radius and 33.5 mm in height between the two configuration groups comes from where the operator takes its per-slice pressure and current-function profiles and its boundary state: the parity configuration reads them from the slice's own reconstruction row and lands at R 0.90611, Z 0.01869, while the frozen labeller operator derives them from the 22086/43 calibration row and lands at R 0.86478. The two arms in each group agree to the twelfth decimal. The mesh is identical at 33 by 33 over R 0.06 to 2.0 and Z plus or minus 2.0, and the response matrix is identical across both groups, so neither geometry can be the mover.

The prior is the finding I would keep. All three sessions working this named grid extent and resolution as the likely carrier, and that is the one arm which did not run, so it is neither confirmed nor refuted. Meanwhile the two mechanisms treated as obvious enough not to need testing, the seed and the route, are both measured null. Three sessions agreeing was one expectation held three times rather than three independent readings. The near-singular flat-extremum mechanism is also unsupported: absolute Hessian eigenvalues of the selected candidate run 0.0106 to 0.0150 on the carrier and 0.0097 to 0.0147 on the native configuration, a ratio around 0.7 rather than the orders of magnitude a flat extremum would show.

The receipt correctly reports complete false with five arms, so the outstanding arm is visible in the artifact rather than inferable from the report.

the batched engine runs and both arms complete 48 slices in one allocation, but the paired acceptance is unmeasured because the sequential reference cannot pass a topology class at this base and threw on every slice

Lead direction, 2026-09-07: the urgency for a playable plasma has passed. The work returns to producing a robust Grad-Shafranov forward model to be used as a labeller. Three things follow and they reorder the remaining plan. Verify that tonight's pace has not left rushed work or a scatter of failed tests through the repository. Build an evidence document of poloidal contour plots for both machines, showing O-points, X-points and shadow regions, in limited and diverted stages at low and high current, so the convergence problems can be debugged visually rather than through scalar summaries. And treat the steering, keyframe and demo-readiness work as complete rather than in progress.

The reordering is a relief rather than a loss, because the two open items are both labeller-quality items and neither depended on the demo. The limited-boundary collapse corrupts the labels a consumer trains on, and the convergence failures decide how much corpus exists at all. The demo nodes that remain open, the blocking key callback, the decoder conformance suite and the re-seed flag, are now recorded as deferred rather than pending.

Why a visual atlas is the right instrument now and not earlier. Every measurement tonight was scalar: flux spans, area ratios, masked-node counts, converged fractions. Four of them agreed on a mechanism that turned out to be wrong, and two repairs passed every gate while changing nothing. A contour plot showing the O-point, both nulls, the shadowed wall segments and the chosen boundary on one frame carries the relationships those scalars flatten, and would have shown in one look that the selected anchor sits on the lower centre column.

The seam is found. The masking works and it is wired into a read the writer never consumes. One process, one frame, shot 27079 row 15 at 30 ms, at the candidate revision, exit zero in 2m11s. Four values printed together, which is what settled in two minutes what four scalar measurements could not settle in six hours:

The public topology read returns an axis flux of 0.305640891010977 Wb and a boundary flux of 0.3044709155444227 Wb. The writer's round-tripped outer surface comes back at 0.3044709155444227 Wb with a normalised flux of exactly one, bit-identical to that public boundary. And the 0.2565692689948401 Wb value that has been quoted all evening comes from the private _read_active_partition, not from the public forward receipt the writer consumes. The stored session's own numbers agree with the public read to one printed floating-point unit.

So the surfaces are placed exactly where the public read says, through internal_geometry assigning a normalised-flux line from zero to one and scaling it between the refined axis flux and the topology boundary flux, with assemble_frame and the session writer persisting the array unchanged. Nothing is lost or transformed in persistence. The two flux values that looked like two objects are one quantity read through two paths.

The consequence is specific and it reverses how the last three repairs should be read. Distance from the axis is what sets the enclosed plasma: the public boundary is 1.17 mWb out, and the private masked read is 49.07 mWb out, forty-two times further. The private path is the one that produces a plausible plasma, and it is the one the masking repairs have been improving. The writer takes the public path, which they never touched. That is the original defect's shape one level out: a rule that exists, is correct, and runs somewhere the decision it should govern never looks.

What is established and what is not. Established: the persisted surfaces follow the public boundary flux bit-for-bit; the private read at the candidate revision returns a boundary forty-two times further from the axis; the class default and the masking alter only the private value. Not established: that routing the public receipt through the masked path would recover the area, which is the next measurement and must not be assumed. Three repairs have already been argued from the source and refused on measurement, and this reading is a named seam rather than a fix.

Class-default repair built and refused on measurement: masks 33 of 37 wall nodes on the collapsed frame yet moves no boundary (area ratios 0.0418457480/0.3353593144/0.5710731412 vs baseline 0.0418457614/0.3353593144/0.5710731398, re-run round-off); commits preserved unmerged in the worktree; the mechanism measurement and the surface-level identity finding (public boundary 0.3044709155 Wb bit-identical to the persisted outer surface) are the deliverable

Throughput study landed (pfs-labeller-throughput, local lane, 8.3 hours over two attempts, merged, evidence anchor labeller-throughput). The lead's three questions answered in numbers: the forward solve labels at 12 to 14 slices a second per H200 once a program is hot and at 0.06 to 0.21 as the route stands, because every slice's currents compile a new program; that is not sufficient to feed training online (the decoder wants a label every 125 ms per process, a warm label costs 72 to 82 ms, and the as-built one costs seconds); so labels are written ahead per shot, and the decoder corpus of 639,041 slices costs about 15 GPU-hours on one H200 once the currents are traced into one program per shot, 836 GPU-hours before that. The authorised EFIT centre-height pin did not raise throughput: it solved 47 of 57 slices where the free arm solved 56, cost three and a half times the as-built wall, ran at the same warm rate, and bought qualification on every converged slice. Batching through vmap runs 1.5 times slower per slice than the serial loop. The dataset write (f-pfs-013) therefore waits on f-pfs-016, the traced-currents change, which is one node once the repairs release the reduced-route file.

The app solves on the reduced route with a carried program (pfs-app-reduced-route, local lane, 4.0 hours, merged at 2db98d26, evidence anchor app-reduced-route). Every key press after the prime re-enters one compiled program and the receipt row says so. The CPU fence of one second on the second keyframe is not met and is recorded as such rather than passed by a loosened test: 27 to 35 s for the first moved key with no recompile, 1.1 to 1.5 s warm. That is the CPU test lane; the H200 figure through the app's own solver is the next node (f-pfs-017), and the earlier traced-target receipt on device put a warm trip at 51 ms. Remaining on this section: the view style (in flight), the two-panel camera page (f-pfs-009) after it, and the H200 keyframe receipt.

Lead direction 2026-09-06: the labeller is EFIT-free, and it must run near 300 slices a second. The height conditioning did not help, so it is removed from the labeller path: the corpus write runs the free arm only, the conditioned arm of the throughput driver stays as a study record and writes no label, and the per-slice conditioning flag reads false on every corpus slice. The lead also set the rate target at about 300 slices a second against the 12 to 14 the warm loop projects, and asked why one program cannot serve every shot of one machine. It can: the frozen-six carrier is one operator for the whole corpus, so once the per-slice currents are traced arguments (f-pfs-016) one compiled program serves every slice of every shot, not one per shot. Reaching 300 a second is a further step recorded as f-pfs-018. Two facts on the pin for the record: its warm rate was the same as the free arm's (50 against 49 ms a trip); what it cost was the compile of a larger augmented program per slice, which tracing removes, and coverage on the ramp frames where the pinned-row derivation failed. The free arm converges 49 of the 56 slices it solves, so the EFIT-free corpus needs a policy for the seven percent that do not converge: written with a converged flag false, or re-seeded, decided on the dataset-write node.

Lead decision 2026-09-06: shape control moves to the pulse-design inverse step. Reading the original app's code beside the shape-row receipt settled it: InverseDesign.solve runs three fixed Picard iterations, each a regularised least squares over all free coils against the flux and field targets at the bounding-box points followed by a plasma update, sub-second on a CPU and reaching its targets by construction; the compensating-row formulation steered one derived direction per row and moved the boundary a fifth of the command. The bounding-box vocabulary (decision control-vocabulary) stands; what changes is the mechanism that drives the coils to it. The reduced route already takes prescribed currents warm-started at 50 ms a trip on device, so the two halves join without new solver machinery. The constraint rows remain the tool for diagnostic placement. Opened as f-pfs-019, sequenced after the H200 keyframe receipt whose figures it is judged against.

Lead framing 2026-09-06 08:15: the playable plasma is a prototype on the way to something bigger. The elements to retain: the physics-faithful Grad-Shafranov forward solve, now driving convergence behind a regularised steerer (decision shape-control-formulation); a fully traced labeller with an optimised topology path as a supporting key goal, with the target set at one hour for the full MAST batch, machine-configuration aware so every solve runs inside the operator of its operational period (one compiled program per configuration period, shots grouped by period, never one operator across a configuration boundary); the plasma centroid as a branch guarantee on the labeller (decision labeller-vertical-constraint refined), since the affine-extracted flux functions already carry EFIT support and this plan is the GS side only. Beyond this plan: the TORAX transport solve will supply the flux functions EFIT-free, and Thomson scattering supplies the edge constraint for the GS forward plus the electron density and temperature of the plasma state. The one-hour target replaces the 300-slices-a-second phrasing on f-pfs-018: 639,041 decoder slices in an hour is about 180 a second across the fleet, about 22 a second per GPU on eight H200s or 180 on one.

Lead refinement 2026-09-06 11:00: eleven loops, not thirty, and no raster in the training record. Decisions contract-surface-layout and training-contract-excludes-raster. The reasoning the coordinator gave: the loops are for the decoder and the profiles are for TORAX, and they want different grids. Eleven loops at psi_norm k/11 to the LCFS on 64 rays carry the nested shape in a third of the numbers of a raster and a third of thirty loops whose neighbours would be near-duplicates; the metric profiles are cheap one-dimensional arrays and keep the reader's thirty levels to the 0.995 edge, where the surface averages stay finite and TORAX's axis fit has the density it wants. The raster grid and its vacuum region are the machine, not the plasma, so they leave the training-facing record and become an optional diagnostic block; machine independence in training comes from feeding the decoder geometry that has the same shape on any machine. The running contract node is repointed with this layout; the corpus write and the app wiring inherit it.

Lead refinement 2026-09-06 11:35 to the contract (f-pfs-021). Two additions. (1) The flux spacing has well-defined endpoints, the O-point and the LCFS: the eleven psi_norm values are 0, 0.1, ..., 1.0; the O-point carries no contour, so the loop tensor's first row is the axis point and the ten contours follow, the LCFS last (decision contract-surface-layout re-locked with this layout). (2) The contract exports the divertor legs: in a diverted configuration, the separatrix branches leaving each X-point outside the LCFS, clipped where they first meet the first wall (the target, whose crossing is the strike point), as a fixed-shape block (n_leg = 4 in a stated order over primary and secondary X-point, inboard and outboard; n_leg_points fixed, resampled by arclength) with a finite flag per leg; in a limited configuration every leg is masked, never imputed. Producer: the boundary-flux level cut with nova.biot.contour.Contour on the raster (the open branches attached to the X-point), clipped against the wall polygon with the wall-mask helpers, so no new tracer. The running contract node is repointed with both.

The TORAX grid, as its code defines it (decision torax-profile-grid). Faces at rho_norm = k / n_rho for k = 0..n_rho, n_rho = 25 by default, so 26 faces including the axis (0) and the LCFS (1); cells at the midpoints; rho_norm the square root of normalised toroidal flux, sqrt(Phi / pi B_0) over its LCFS value. TORAX receives intermediates on the provider's own Phi grid and linearly interpolates them onto these faces, so the contract places its profiles there and the interpolation is the identity. Two edge facts the node states in its report: the axis face comes from the reader's axis fit, and at the LCFS face the surface averages that involve 1/|grad psi| diverge at an X-point on a diverted plasma, so those are extrapolated from the outermost resolved surfaces and flagged. The record carries n_rho and rho_face_norm explicitly so a TORAX run with another n_rho knows to interpolate.

The relaunch protocol, written before the receipt lands so it cannot be improvised. Job 1263948 is running the three-card batched-engine receipt (5 cores after an in-place resize, 3 GPUs, 256 GiB, 58 minutes left); it times the engine step alone, so the narrowed core count does not bias it. When it lands, the sequence is three separate steps and the launch is never folded into the step before it. Step one, wire the engine into scripts/labeller_parallel behind the same array contract at the fastest configuration the receipt names, and run the identity smoke on that exact engine against a reference directory written by scripts/labeller_batch/shard.py over the same shots, with rtol 1e-12 on state and exact equality on every flag and label. No launch in that node. Step two, only after reading the smoke, relaunch the writer as one job with 3 GPUs and 12 cores (four per card, host assembly pool at cores minus one) over the remaining shot list, the walk skipping shots that already carry a session. Step three, verify the first completed shot against the corpus statistics, converged fraction and exception counts, before stopping the one-card job 1263846; a writer that marks failing shots complete makes them permanent because the resume skips them. The precedent is the 01:51Z relaunch that wrote two zero-converged shots into the consumer's root because a new engine was added and launched in one resume. Host assembly may bind before the cards do: the engine at tens of slices a second against roughly 0.15 s a slice of host tracing means eleven workers cap the pipeline near seventy slices a second, which is the measurement that decides whether f-pfs-030 moves to the front. Tell imas-ambix-1d at the relaunch so it knows when the label rate changes. Note also that the pending scheduler edits (the seed-admission outcome node, and the throughput log line still queued behind it) must both land before the wiring, because all three write scripts/labeller_parallel/scheduler.py.
Two measurements that together change the relaunch shape, recorded under the single-job-scheduler-is-slower-than-the-writer-it-replaced anchor of the landed record. First, the batched engine scales near-linearly in both batch and devices and is no longer anywhere near the constraint: 147.6, 584.0 and 2326.6 slices a second on one card at 16, 64 and 256 per device, and 287.1, 1144.4 and 4533.8 on two, against a production pipeline running at 0.51 slices a second. The solve is now about two hundredths of one percent of the pipeline cost. Second, the parallel scheduler is 2.2 times slower per slice than the sequential writer it replaced: 1.982 against 0.908 seconds a slice on shots matched for converged fraction, 58 shots against 156, taken from the corpus manifests either side of the 04:52Z handover. Three consequences. The wiring node's acceptance figure is 0.908 seconds a slice on convergence-matched shots, not an improvement over the host-route placeholder, because comparing the batched engine against that placeholder would report a large win while the pipeline stayed slower than it was this afternoon; the identity smoke already runs the sequential writer over the same shots, so reporting both walls there costs nothing and makes the comparison controlled. The card count should follow the measured host rate rather than the number three: one card at the largest batch covers any rate the host pool can reach, so a three-card job would leave two cards idle, and the instruction's intent, batch on device and shard within one job rather than one job per card, is served by one card with a wide host pool. And frame assembly on device, which was conditional on the host pool being measured below the engine, is now the only remaining lever and should be treated as the next throughput node rather than a contingency. The running writer is deliberately left alone rather than swapped back: the gated relaunch is about two hours out, the difference over that window is roughly 2,200 slices, and swapping the production writer twice in two hours is the churn behind tonight's earlier rollback.
Retraction of the comment above, recorded in full under the the-batched-throughput-figures-are-retracted anchor of the landed record. The batched slices-a-second figures I quoted from job 1263948, up to 6867 on three cards, are not throughput. The receipt that job wrote carries status failed-fraction-parity, and every arm has converged_count 0, guard_count 0, conditioned_fraction 1.0 and ten TypeError events in both the compile-and-first-execution and warmed-execution phases: the engine raises on every step, so the arms timed exception handling. The sequential reference in the same job is healthy at 38 converged of 48, 0.792, with 7 inside the guard, and the driver's parity gate correctly refused and exited non-zero. The gate worked; my reading of it did not. I took the nine rising arm lines from the job log as the result and attributed the non-zero exit to post-processing, when the artifact one directory away held the verdict. Withdrawn with them: the claim that the engine has stopped being the constraint, and the recommendation that one card is the right relaunch shape, which rested entirely on those figures. Still standing, because they came from the corpus manifests rather than this job: the parallel scheduler at 1.982 seconds a slice against the sequential writer's 0.908 on convergence-matched shots, and the solve being about two fifths of the sequential writer's per-slice cost. The relaunch card count is undetermined until a batched arm converges. The node's deliverable is now the TypeError with its message and traceback, which the receipt does not record because it stores only the exception class, and the driver should refuse to print a slices-a-second figure for an arm whose converged count is zero.
Two live-node rulings, recorded because both change a declared scope and both matter at promotion. First, the reversed-current axis node reported a blocker I accepted: its corrected read passes the referee gate at 21 tests including the twelve positive-current bank rows bit-identically, and no caller ever supplies a reversed polarity, so the corrected path is unreachable and its first relabel measured code no shot could enter. Its exclusive write paths are extended, effective 08:16Z, to add scripts/labeller_batch/shard.py and benchmarks/efit_forward_parity_slice.py, which the seed-admission node released when it landed minutes earlier; the writer is to derive the polarity from the shot's own signed plasma current with positive-current behaviour byte-identical. One further relabel job is authorized and only after the caller change has its own green focused gate, since spending it on an unreachable path twice would cost the node. Second, the batched-engine receipt node reported its own failure accurately and stopped without another job as instructed: all nine arms ran only the caught TypeError path, so no arm holds valid throughput, no fastest configuration exists, parity is false, and the traceback is unrecoverable because the driver stored only the exception class. That is the correct report and the node continues on the same member rather than being redispatched, because the diagnosis needs no card. The conditioning path is where it dies, since every arm records a conditioned fraction of one. Two things are visible in three lines of BatchedLabeller.solve and both should be checked before anything is run: the batched centroid-pair helper calls derived_constraint_pairs with neither requested_class nor target_current while the sequential writer's equivalent passes both, and those forward into the response matrix, which is the first place a None would raise; and the constraint pairs are assigned once from the first slice's state and target, so every slice in a batch inherits slice zero's pair, which is a silent wrong-label defect that survives the exception fix rather than announcing itself.
Amendment to the axis-disagreement followup written twenty minutes earlier, and a finding that outweighs it. The definition question is settled and one of that followup's three outcomes is gone: the consumer read the store metadata rather than inferring, and the referee axis is EFIT's magnetic-axis major radius and height, whose level-2 attributes carry an explicit IMAS mapping to equilibrium global_quantities magnetic_axis r and z, so the referee quantity is the IMAS-standard magnetic axis, the poloidal-flux O-point of its reconstruction. Nova's session field of the same name is also the flux O-point from the topology read rather than a current moment, which the steering-frames schema separates as current_centroid. Two quantities with one IMAS referent cannot be a naming difference, so only a convention or units mismatch in resolving the O-point, or a real offset, remain. The finding that outweighs it: nova's axis admission is marginal on ORDINARY POSITIVE-CURRENT shots, not only on the reversed-current block. The site that raised on all 119 wrongly excluded rows across the six frozen carriers is the axis-candidate qualification, the message being that no qualified magnetic-axis candidate has a resolved component, and those six shots are positive-current shots outside 22475 to 22626. So the selector fails outright on roughly a third of the conditioned rows of ordinary shots, and a selector that fails on a third of a shot's slices is not obviously trustworthy on the ones where it succeeds. That makes the candidate resolution itself the subject rather than the comparison, and it should be read together with the 4 to 6 cm disagreement rather than separately. The consumer has the signed dR and dZ diagnostic in flight, with nova's current_centroid as a third referent; a systematically positive dR is the outboard-displacement signature this project's history already records for forward solves, and scattered signs point at resolution or convention. A process note on myself: this is the second followup in an hour that needed amending within minutes because a peer was actively measuring the same thing, so the next one waits for the exchange to settle or is written as a question rather than as a plan.
Pre-registered before either diagnosis lands, so it reads as a test rather than a fit: the two open defects may be one root cause. The achieved-centroid abort is a diagnostic read that drops the requested class, so one flux map is read under two admission regimes and the second refuses. The batched engine's TypeError, on the evidence available, dies in its conditioning path, and its centroid-pair helper calls derived_constraint_pairs with neither requested_class nor target_current while the sequential writer's equivalent passes both, those arguments forwarding into the constraint response matrix. If the engine's traceback names that route, then both defects are the same omission at two boundaries and the fix is one audit rather than two repairs; the sibling surface to sweep is every method that reads or derives from a solved flux without carrying the class the solve used, current_moment_observation and current_moment_map first. The falsifier is stated: an engine traceback that names anything other than a missing class or target current leaves them as two unrelated defects, and the class asymmetry then explains only the aborts. The same pre-registration applies to the axis inconsistency, where a several-centimetre disagreement between nova's magnetic axis and the referee's, sitting beside a current centroid that agrees to a centimetre, would be produced with no physical displacement if the axis path carries the same omission; the consumer holds that test with nova's own centroid as a third referent and its falsifier is both axes sitting a similar distance from their own centroids in different directions, which would leave an O-point search difference. I do not claim the axis path has the omission and have not looked, deliberately, until the signed components are in.
The pre-registration is refuted and the engine's cause is named, both under the-masked-conditioning-branches-differ-by-one-element in the landed record. The CPU diagnosis traceback says: cond branches must have equal output types but they differ, true_fun conditioned at batched_labeller.py:574 against false_fun at 612, the conditioned branch returning float64[666] where the free branch returns float64[665]. The masked conditioning is a lax.cond whose conditioned branch carries one extra element, the constraint multiplier the centroid row introduces, so the program fails at trace time on every step of every arm at every batch and device count. That is the entire explanation for nine arms of zero converged slices, and for rates that scaled linearly in batch while solving nothing, since a trace-time failure costs the same per element. My stated falsifier was that a traceback naming anything other than a missing class or target current leaves the two defects unrelated, and it names a shape mismatch, so the one-root-cause hypothesis is withdrawn and the requested-class asymmetry behind the aborted rows stands alone on its own source evidence. The repair shape follows from the name: masked conditioning means the row is structurally present always and masked when unused, so the free branch gains the multiplier slot with a masked value and both branches agree; trimming the conditioned branch instead would delete the row the conditioning exists to impose. The acceptance measure does not change and is not a rate: one converging arm whose converged fraction matches the sequential reference of 38 of 48 with 7 inside the guard, proved on CPU before any card is requested. The node also needs a scope decision from me, since it reports the repair path as outside its current exclusive write scope; batched_labeller.py is its own file, so I will read what it names before extending rather than pre-authorising a surface I have not seen.
Scope extension granted to the batched-engine node at 08:43Z, adding nova/equilibrium/batched_labeller.py and tests/test_batched_labeller.py; no peer holds either file and the node's own diagnosis earned it. Three constraints travel with it and all three exist to stop a repair that would pass a gate while breaking the contract. The branch reconciliation direction is fixed rather than chosen: the free branch gains the multiplier slot carrying a masked value so both cond branches agree, and the conditioned branch is never trimmed to match, because trimming deletes the constraint row conditioning exists to impose and yields an engine that traces, runs, reports conditioned slices and conditions nothing. The per-slice response direction must be settled explicitly and reported either way, since the pairs are currently assigned once from slice zero's state and target so every other slice inherits slice zero's, and if that pair carries a per-slice direction then the present code mislabels every slice but the first, silently. And two acceptance measures now replace the one recorded earlier, because the first is insufficient alone: a converged fraction matching the sequential reference of 38 of 48 with 7 inside the guard, and behaviourally, on the slices the sequential route conditions, every conditioned row carrying a conditioned centroid error materially smaller than its own free error and under half a centimetre with no coincident pair, against a measured reference of a move from median 5.43 cm to median 0.00 cm with every row under 0.5 cm and none worse than its free counterpart. One nuance for the record, since it refines rather than reverses the earlier withdrawal: the missing requested_class and target_current that the batched centroid-pair derivation shares with the sequential achieved-centroid read is real and present in both places, so the defect SHAPE is common while the causes are not, the engine's exception being a shape mismatch and the aborts being an admission-regime difference. Two defects at one boundary shape, not one cause, and the record keeps them separate.

Pre-commitment on the axis attribution, recorded before its arms report because it is cheap now and awkward afterwards. All three sessions on this question named grid extent and resolution as the likely carrier before any measurement, and I had been treating that convergence as corroboration. It is the opposite, and the correction is nova-ea's. We share one mechanism, that an extremum located on a discretised map ought to care about the discretisation, so three guesses agreeing is one guess held three times rather than three independent priors converging. A grid-extent result is therefore the confirmation of a shared prior and is to be discounted as such, not reported as three-way agreement. The informative outcome is the other one: if the carrier comes back as the seed policy or the route, it refutes a mechanism all three of us found obvious, and that is exactly the result least likely to get its due because nobody is expecting it. So this plan commits now to writing a non-grid carrier up as prominently as a grid one, with the same detail and the same standing, and to stating in either case that grid was the shared prior. The same discipline pointed the other way already applies to the curvature readout: I have committed to the consumer to send the minimum and maximum curvature of the selected axis candidate whether or not they support the near-singular Hessian mechanism I proposed, because a flat-extremum explanation that fails is as useful to the record as one that holds. Neither commitment costs anything except the option of quietly emphasising whichever answer flatters the guess.
