What is defective is the record, and specifically an auditor asking which node touched shard.py would find nothing. Practice change for the rest of this session and worth carrying: pass every commit a node landed to the promotion rather than its tip, since the tip is what merges and the list is what the ledger keeps, and name each path with a reason whenever a scope was extended mid-flight. The tooling catches the second only if the first is done properly, which is the part neither the guard nor I had connected.
Priority change, and it is the largest of the night. The limited-boundary defect is not a label-quality item, it corrupts the consumer's model input. Checked against its source rather than recalled, the consumer reads the flux surfaces, the axis, the X-points, the diverted flag and derived shape scalars, and consumes no TORAX faces and no p_prime or ff_prime, so the compressed-abscissa half of the warning does not reach it at all. The half that does reach it is the whole conditioning image: the outermost surface is the boundary and the nested set is rendered to a 64 by 64 raster as the model's conditioning. So on a corrupted frame the model is shown a small blob in open space and trained to emit a camera frame of a plasma filling the vessel, which is a systematically wrong input paired with a correct target, and that is the shape that teaches a model to ignore its conditioning. The consumer's most conspicuous failure is that its decoder looks weakly conditioned, so this is a live candidate for the prototype's failure mode rather than housekeeping. Its derived shape scalars, minor radius, elongation and triangularity, are contaminated by the same boundary. Three consequences recorded. The repair is now the highest-value item on this plan, above the demo measurements and far above throughput. The consumer is measuring exposure by geometry, minimum distance from the outermost surface to the limiter and enclosed area, rather than by the class label, which is right because the collapse is worst at the first guarded frame and recovers monotonically, so severity varies widely within the limited class and a class filter would drop good frames and keep bad ones. And a warning that is mine to give because it is a property of my schema: flux_surface_psi_norm cannot reveal this and must not be used as a check, since it is normalised between axis and boundary by construction and runs cleanly from zero to one on a frame whose physical span has collapsed by ninety-nine per cent. The nesting looks well formed while representing a region twenty-four times too small, so the two contamination paths, wrong extent and compressed radial coordinate, are separable only in physical coordinates. Anything normalised sees neither.
Two operational notes and one likely collapse of a finding into an existing fix. First the collapse: the consumer measured 119 of 371 semantically admitted slices on the six carriers with no finite vertices at all on the outermost flux level, absent rather than displaced, and asked whether it is the limited-branch defect wearing another face. It is almost certainly neither that nor new. It had earlier measured 119 rows on the same six shots carrying an exception with zero conditioned trips out of 372 free-converged, and two 119s on one population with denominators differing by one is not a coincidence to bet against. Those rows abort at the achieved-centroid read before the frame geometry completes, so the outermost surface is never traced and its vertices are absent. If the index sets intersect fully, the second symptom collapses into the class repair already in flight rather than opening a defect, and the consumer can test it without a job because it holds both sets. Recorded now so the prediction is falsifiable rather than fitted. Second, a lane constraint that has now cost four nodes and belongs in every brief that measures on CPU: the debug partition's one-hour cap is the binding constraint on CPU verification of device programs, and the shape-regularisation node, the vertical-mode repair twice, and the batched engine's 48-slice acceptance arm have each been killed by it rather than by anything they were measuring. A CPU arm must be sized to fit the hour when the brief is written, not discovered to exceed it. Third and consequently, the batched engine's acceptance measure needs restating at a scale that fits: rather than matching a sequential reference of 38 converged of 48 with 7 guarded, the measure becomes a sampled subset where the batched converged fraction matches the sequential route on the same subset and every conditioned row carries a centroid error materially smaller than its own free error and under half a centimetre. That is scale-free, keeps both halves that a single-sided measure would let a wrong repair pass, and leaves the full 48-slice parity for one card once convergence is proven cheaply.

§ Decisions

control-vocabulary

Lead decision 2026-09-05: flux and field constraints at the bounding-box points that move the separatrix turning points, with the null row at the X-point and gap rows at the wall, supersede the Miller-parametrised isoflux target whose fit to a diverted boundary made neither steering arm converge. Restored 2026-09-05 19:35 after the first structured write dropped the plan's original decision markup.

compensating-current-direction

Lead decision 2026-09-05: derived directions on the machine's active mapping now; the collateral-penalised derivation with a second row held still once the shape rows exist. Restored 2026-09-05 19:35 after the first structured write dropped the plan's original decision markup.

serving-compute

Lead direction 2026-09-05: the compute runs on the H200 reservation when live, with the controls and playback streamed over the tunnel connecting a local client with the ITER remote, the way the reckon docs server is reached today. The imas-codex vLLM target already binds a forward to a compute node discovered through squeue, so no relay is needed on the login node.

tunnel-ownership

Lead direction 2026-09-05: the tunnels are managed in the imas-codex project. Requested from the imas-codex live coordinator on 2026-09-05 with the proposed port 18506 and job name nova-playable; nova's launcher matches whatever that project settles on.

plasma-view-style

Lead direction 2026-09-05 22:40: follow the style of the existing pulse-design plasma rendering and of imas-ink without reproducing either exactly; no bounding boxes or axes on the plasma plot; simple coil outlines and the first-wall profile; light purple plasma hex cells with the edge cells rendered clipped as they are solved; dark grey contour lines, not filled; the primary topological points (X-point, O-point) indicated.

camera-window-layout

Lead direction 2026-09-05 23:00 asked for a second window for the decoded MAST video supported by the flux-surface solve. One document keeps the two panels on the one keyframe and the one tunnelled port; the camera-only view argument gives the literal second window on another screen; the dotted-path plug-in keeps the coupled-repository rule one way (imas-ambix consumes nova, nova never imports imas-ambix) and lets the layout, keys and recording work before the decoder exists. Proposed to the imas-ambix coordinator on 2026-09-05 with two open questions: whether decode takes one frame or a short history, and the rbb image shape and rate.

labeller-vertical-constraint

Lead direction 2026-09-06 08:15, refining the morning's removal: the forward solves are already supported by the affine-extracted flux functions, so EFIT labels already stand behind every solve, and a plasma centroid measurement that guarantees the correct branch makes sense. The centroid row is therefore kept on the labeller as a branch guard, flagged per slice with its target source as before; its cost was never the warm rate (the same 50 ms a trip) but the per-slice compile, which the traced-currents change removes, and coverage on the ramp frames, which the dataset write records as unconverged rather than dropping. The EFIT-free labeller is the destination, reached when the TORAX transport solve supplies the flux functions and Thomson supplies the edge constraint and the plasma state; this decision covers the GS side only.

decoder-input-is-physics-state

Lead clarification 2026-09-05 23:05: imas-ambix does the training, nova supplies the labels, nova does not import imas-ambix, and the decoder works from the flux map, the LCFS, the divertor strike points, the O-point and the X-points, never from pixels. The FrameDecoder seam takes one SteeringFrame in. Facts from the imas-ambix code verified by a peer session: native decoder output 256 by 256 grayscale (sensor aspect 112 by 156), playback constant 8 frames per second, no live holder for that plan tonight; the lead resumes the conversation with a new imas-ambix worker once nova's capabilities are measured.

How does the playable solve steer the plasma shape: augmented constraint rows with a compensating unknown per row, or the pulse-design inverse step that solves all free coil currents from the shape targets and lets the forward solve respond?

Lead decision 2026-09-06 after reading the shape-row receipt beside the pulse-design code: the original app steers the boundary sub-second on a CPU by a Tikhonov-regularised least squares over all free coils against flux and field targets at the bounding-box points, followed by a plasma update; the compensating-row formulation steered one direction per row through a four-and-a-half-decade spectrum and moved the boundary a fifth of the command. Shape control therefore uses the inverse step on the response matrix, with the reduced route solving forward on the prescribed currents warm-started; the constraint rows stay for diagnostic placement (centroid, Thomson crossing). The running steering-authority measurement finishes as the record of why.

What does the EFIT-free labeller corpus do with the slices the free solve does not converge (about one in eight of those it solves)?

Lead decision 2026-09-06: every slice is written with its converged flag and terminal residual so imas-ambix decides what it trains on; a camera frame is never left without a label row, and nothing is dropped silently.

What does the labeller contract (the steering-frame record and its netCDF session store) carry of the plasma's internal geometry, beyond the raster, the separatrix and the LCFS it carries today?

Lead direction 2026-09-06 10:35: the contract is the sanctioned carrier of the prototype's data to imas-ambix and must carry the plasma's internal geometry, not only its boundary: a set of nested flux surfaces at about thirty discrete values of psi_norm, the psi_norm and the actual psi values passed back, the R, Z contour coordinates of each surface in a fixed shape, and all the flux-surface metrics TORAX uses, since the TORAX transport solve is the destination that will supply the flux functions EFIT-free. Nova already computes these (FluxSurfaceGeometry on the host, traced_flux_surface_geometry on device); the contract exposes them, it does not re-derive them.

How does the playable view cut its contour lines?

Lead direction 2026-09-06 10:35: do not re-invent a contourer for the display; the original Bokeh surface has one implemented in nova (nova.biot.contour.Contour on contourpy, reached through LevelSet.contour in the pulse-design app) and it changes only if performance dictates, with a measurement. The view-style landing wrote its own marching squares in apps/pulsedesign/poloidal_view.py; that is removed. The display draws the contract's surfaces directly once the frame carries them, so the display and the labeller show the same geometry.

Does the prototype move its data through the labeller contract, or through channel dictionaries read off the equilibrium?

Lead direction 2026-09-06 10:35: the prototype passes the majority of its data using the sanctioned labeller contract, which is designed and largely implemented as the data persisted for imas-ambix. Today neither the app (frame_push reads the equilibrium directly) nor the labeller driver (per-slice JSON) goes through assemble_frame or write_session; both move onto the contract, so what a person sees in the view is what the decoder is trained on.

How many nested surfaces does the contract carry as fixed-shape loops, on what levels, and do the TORAX metric profiles share that grid?

Lead 2026-09-06 11:00 and 11:35: eleven discrete values of psi_norm with well-defined endpoints, the O-point at psi_norm 0 and the LCFS at 1, so the levels are linspace(0, 1, 11) with spacing 0.1. There is no contour at the O-point, which is a point: row 0 of the fixed-shape loop tensor (11, 64, 2) is the magnetic axis repeated on every ray (flux_surface_psi[0] the axis flux), rows 1 to 10 are the traced surfaces at 0.1 to 1.0, row 10 the LCFS. The TORAX metric profiles keep their own thirty-level grid from the axis floor to the 0.995 edge where the surface averages stay finite. The tracer integrates on 128 rays; 64 is the storage.

Is the per-pixel raster (psi, psi_norm and the domain label on the machine grid) part of the decoder-facing contract?

Lead direction 2026-09-06 11:00: the contract exists to avoid passing the per-pixel flux map; imas-ambix trains better off a fixed-shape plasma geometry; the vacuum-field contours are neither needed nor wanted as training input; and this contract is what makes the training machine-independent, since a machine grid and its vacuum region are the machine while nested surfaces, landmarks and profiles are the plasma. The raster block stays available as a diagnostic for playback and debugging, written only on request and flagged so, never a training input.

On what radial grid does the contract carry the TORAX metric profiles?

Lead 2026-09-06 11:40: if the TORAX grid has 25 cells, exchange on the same grid with no interpolation effort. TORAX's definition, read from its checkout (torax/_src/torax_pydantic/interpolated_param_2d.py Grid1D and torax/_src/geometry/standard_geometry.py): the mesh is a one-dimensional grid of faces, face_centers = linspace(0, 1, n_rho + 1) which must include 0 and 1, cell centres the midpoints, n_rho = 25 by default so 26 faces at k/25; the radial label is rho_norm = sqrt(Phi / (pi B_0)) / rho_b with rho_b that value at the last provided point, the LCFS; a geometry provider hands StandardGeometryIntermediates on its own Phi grid and TORAX interpolates every quantity with np.interp onto its faces, cells and a high-resolution grid. So a record whose Phi grid reproduces rho_norm = k/25 exactly makes the face interpolation the identity. Nova's FluxSurfaceGeometry already accepts a requested rho_tor_norm grid, so the profiles are placed on TORAX's faces at the source. This supersedes the thirty-level profile grid of contract-surface-layout; the eleven psi_norm loops for the decoder are unchanged.

labeller-execution-shape

Lead 2026-09-06 19:00Z on seeing 192 one-hour shard jobs at 55 to 60 percent card utilisation: separate jobs per shard are sloppy; a single job over all free H200 cards (3 tonight, 4 to 8 when the serve is down) must batch slices from many shots into one compiled program per step and shard that batch across devices with the operator replicated, so the per-slice host loop and the per-job compile disappear and the cards saturate. The corpus writer is one job per corpus run, resumable by shot; one job per card was an interim and is not the design. Host-side frame assembly is pipelined behind the device stage and moves on device if it binds. Batching order: the compiled slice program (landed, main c04515ad) is the element; vmap over shots within a device; NamedSharding over a batch mesh across devices; conditioning is a masked second pass on device.

conditioned-slices-for-demo

Agreed between the nova and imas-ambix sessions on 2026-09-06 (18:33Z to 19:30Z) from the guard-conditioning smoke on shot 22056: the free solve sits within 50 mm of the EFIT current centroid on 21 of 61 slices and conditioning (one re-solve with the vertical current-centroid row imposed at efm/current_centrd_z, triggered only when the free solve failed the guard or did not converge) raises that to 47 of 61. Excluding conditioned slices is not a random exclusion: they are the hard equilibria, so free-only training carries a selection bias correlated with difficulty. Rule: a slice with conditioned == true (the per-slice manifest and companion field) is admissible as a training label for the demo and for any capability work not scored against the EFIT referee; any result scored against the EFIT referee trains on conditioned == false slices only, because the conditioned label carries an EFIT reading. Consumers also filter on converged == true (unconverged slices are written, flagged, never dropped) and treat qualified (terminal residual within 1e-10) as a strict policy constant, not a quality gate. The corpus written tonight under labeller_sessions/76906a29 stays in this layout and this root for the remainder of the run so the demo reads one root.
